From the dialogue so far and your current candidates, ask the one follow-up question that best separates them.\n\n[Current Turn]\n10\n\n[Current Candidates]\nF22, F23, F25.1\n\n[Dialogue History]\nDoctor: Hello, welcome to the clinic. What brings you in to see me today?\n\nPatient: I came in because... well, I keep hearing people talking about me. It's usually board members or my staff. I hear them through the conference room walls, or even from my phone when it's completely turned off. They're always saying things like I'm ruining the company. It sounds so incredibly clear that I'll actually answer them out loud... and then I realize no one else in the room heard a thing.\n\nDoctor: Do you believe that the voices you're hearing are real?\n\nPatient: Yes. I mean, like I just mentioned, they sound completely real to me. When I hear the board members or my staff through the conference room walls, or coming from my phone even when it's turned off, there's no difference between that and a normal conversation. \n\nI hear them saying I'm ruining the company, and the voices are so clear that I actually answer them back. It's only after I speak out loud and look around to realize no one else in the room heard a thing that I get confused. But in the moment... yes, they feel absolutely real.\n\nDoctor: Do you believe the board members or your staff are actually plotting against you or trying to harm you in any way?\n\nPatient: No, I don't think they're plotting against me or trying to hurt me. I mean... I haven't noticed anything like that. They just say those things about how I'm ruining the company, but I don't feel like they're planning a conspiracy or anything dangerous. It's just... the talking.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever find it hard to express your thoughts or have trouble organizing your thoughts when talking to others?\n\nPatient: No, I haven't noticed anything like that. I mean... I talk to people all day for my job, directing my staff and running meetings. I don't have any trouble putting my sentences together or explaining what I want to say. My own thoughts are perfectly clear to me... it's really just hearing those voices that's the issue.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any changes in your behavior or the way you act that seem unusual or out of character for you?\n\nPatient: Yes, I actually have. It's... well, it happens mostly in meetings, or sometimes at our gallery events. I'll just suddenly stand up. And I'll start rearranging things on the table—like putting pens or people's name cards into these specific patterns. \n\nOr, um, someone will say something, and I'll just repeat the exact phrase they just said out loud. Sometimes I even hold my hands up in the air, like I'm presenting something to the room, but I'm not holding anything. In the moment I just do it, but afterward... I mean, I know it makes absolutely no sense for the situation. It's definitely very out of character for me.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever feel like your thoughts are racing or jumping from one idea to another in a way that's hard to control?\n\nPatient: No, not really. I mean, my thoughts don't feel like they're racing or jumping around out of control. Like I said before, my own thoughts feel pretty clear to me. It's just the voices that get in the way, but my actual thinking process... it feels normal. I don't feel like I'm losing control of my own ideas.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever find it hard to stay on topic when you're talking to someone?\n\nPatient: No, I don't think so. I mean, I feel like I stay on topic just fine. When I'm talking to my staff or anyone else, I know what I want to say and I just say it. I haven't noticed any issues with getting sidetracked or anything like that. Like I said, my own thinking and talking seem pretty normal to me.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever have thoughts that don't make sense to you or seem disconnected from each other?\n\nPatient: No, not really. My thoughts feel connected. I don't have moments where my own ideas don't make sense to me. The things I think about are normal... it's just the other things, like the voices, that don't make sense. But my actual thoughts are fine.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed doing anything else out of character or unusual besides rearranging things and repeating phrases in meetings and events?\n\nPatient: Well, besides what we just talked about... sometimes I'll just suddenly stand up in the middle of a meeting. Or I'll hold my hands up in the air, kind of like I'm presenting something to everyone. It's strange because, afterward, I know it made absolutely no sense for the room or what was going on. But in the moment, I just do it.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Ask about exactly ONE thing. When I hear the board members or my staff through the conference room walls, or coming from my phone even when it's turned off, there's no difference between that and a normal conversation. \n\nI hear them saying I'm ruining the company, and the voices are so clear that I actually answer them back. Sometimes I even hold my hands up in the air, like I'm presenting something to the room, but I'm not holding anything. In the moment I just do it, but afterward... I mean, I know it makes absolutely no sense for the situation. It's definitely very out of character for me.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever feel like your thoughts are racing or jumping from one idea to another in a way that's hard to control?\n\nPatient: No, not really. I mean, my thoughts don't feel like they're racing or jumping around out of control. Like I said before, my own thoughts feel pretty clear to me. It's just the voices that get in the way, but my actual thinking process... it feels normal. I don't feel like I'm losing control of my own ideas.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever find it hard to stay on topic when you're talking to someone?\n\nPatient: No, I don't think so. I mean, I feel like I stay on topic just fine. When I'm talking to my staff or anyone else, I know what I want to say and I just say it. I haven't noticed any issues with getting sidetracked or anything like that. Like I said, my own thinking and talking seem pretty normal to me.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever have thoughts that don't make sense to you or seem disconnected from each other?\n\nPatient: No, not really. My thoughts feel connected. I don't have moments where my own ideas don't make sense to me. The things I think about are normal... it's just the other things, like the voices, that don't make sense. But my actual thoughts are fine.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed doing anything else out of character or unusual besides rearranging things and repeating phrases in meetings and events?\n\nPatient: Well, besides what we just talked about... sometimes I'll just suddenly stand up in the middle of a meeting. Or I'll hold my hands up in the air, kind of like I'm presenting something to everyone. It's strange because, afterward, I know it made absolutely no sense for the room or what was going on. But in the moment, I just do it.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever have trouble understanding what's being said to you because your mind is somewhere else?\n\nPatient: No, I don't think so. When people are talking to me, I hear what they're saying. I might be distracted by... well, the other things going on, but I still understand the words they're saying to me. My mind doesn't really drift off like that.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever have thoughts that feel like they're not connected to each other in a logical way?\n\nPatient: No, I don't think so. I mean, you kind of just asked me that, right? My thoughts feel fine. They connect to each other the way they always have. I haven't noticed any issues with getting sidetracked or anything like that. Like I said, my own thinking and talking seem pretty normal to me.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever have thoughts that don't make sense to you or seem disconnected from each other?\n\nPatient: No, not really. My thoughts feel connected. I don't have moments where my own ideas don't make sense to me. The things I think about are normal... it's just the other things, like the voices, that don't make sense. But my actual thoughts are fine.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed doing anything else out of character or unusual besides rearranging things and repeating phrases in meetings and events?\n\nPatient: Well, besides what we just talked about... sometimes I'll just suddenly stand up in the middle of a meeting. Or I'll hold my hands up in the air, kind of like I'm presenting something to everyone. It's strange because, afterward, I know it made absolutely no sense for the room or what was going on. But in the moment, I just do it.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever have trouble understanding what's being said to you because your mind is somewhere else?\n\nPatient: No, I don't think so. When people are talking to me, I hear what they're saying. I might be distracted by... well, the other things going on, but I still understand the words they're saying to me. My mind doesn't really drift off like that.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever have thoughts that feel like they're not connected to each other in a logical way?\n\nPatient: No, I don't think so. I mean, you kind of just asked me that, right? My thoughts feel fine. They connect to each other the way they always have. It's just... the voices and the things I do that are strange, not the thoughts themselves.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever have difficulty following your own train of thought?\n\nPatient: No, I don't have trouble with that. If I start thinking about a problem at work or... whatever it is, I can follow it through to the end just fine. My train of thought is clear to me. Like I said, it's really just the other things happening around me that are the issue, not my actual thinking.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever have thoughts that feel like they're being taken out of your mind or broadcasted to others?\n\nPatient: No, I've never experienced anything like that. My thoughts are my own... they stay in my head. I don't feel like anyone is taking them or broadcasting them for other people to hear. That's not something that happens to me.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Ask about exactly ONE thing. From the dialogue so far and your candidates from the previous turn, list the disorders this patient could have.\n\n[Current Turn]\n15\n\n[Previous Candidates]\nF22, F23, F25.1\n\n[Dialogue History]\nDoctor: Hello, welcome to the clinic. What brings you in to see me today?\n\nPatient: I came in because... well, I keep hearing people talking about me. It's usually board members or my staff. I hear them through the conference room walls, or even from my phone when it's completely turned off. They're always saying things like I'm ruining the company. It sounds so incredibly clear that I'll actually answer them out loud... and then I realize no one else in the room heard a thing.\n\nDoctor: Do you believe that the voices you're hearing are real?\n\nPatient: Yes. I mean, like I just mentioned, they sound completely real to me. When I hear the board members or my staff through the conference room walls, or coming from my phone even when it's turned off, there's no difference between that and a normal conversation. \n\nI hear them saying I'm ruining the company, and the voices are so clear that I actually answer them back. It's only after I speak out loud and look around to realize no one else in the room heard a thing that I get confused. But in the moment... yes, they feel absolutely real.\n\nDoctor: Do you believe the board members or your staff are actually plotting against you or trying to harm you in any way?\n\nPatient: No, I don't think they're plotting against me or trying to hurt me. I mean... I haven't noticed anything like that. They just say those things about how I'm ruining the company, but I don't feel like they're planning a conspiracy or anything dangerous. It's just... the talking.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever find it hard to express your thoughts or have trouble organizing your thoughts when talking to others?\n\nPatient: No, I haven't noticed anything like that. I mean... I talk to people all day for my job, directing my staff and running meetings. I don't have any trouble putting my sentences together or explaining what I want to say. My own thoughts are perfectly clear to me... it's really just hearing those voices that's the issue.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any changes in your behavior or the way you act that seem unusual or out of character for you?\n\nPatient: Yes, I actually have. It's... well, it happens mostly in meetings, or sometimes at our gallery events. I'll just suddenly stand up. And I'll start rearranging things on the table—like putting pens or people's name cards into these specific patterns. \n\nOr, um, someone will say something, and I'll just repeat the exact phrase they just said out loud. Sometimes I even hold my hands up in the air, like I'm presenting something to the room, but I'm not holding anything. In the moment I just do it, but afterward... I mean, I know it makes absolutely no sense for the situation. It's definitely very out of character for me.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever feel like your thoughts are racing or jumping from one idea to another in a way that's hard to control?\n\nPatient: No, not really. I mean, my thoughts don't feel like they're racing or jumping around out of control. Like I said before, my own thoughts feel pretty clear to me. It's just the voices that get in the way, but my actual thinking process... it feels normal. I don't feel like I'm losing control of my own ideas.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever find it hard to stay on topic when you're talking to someone?\n\nPatient: No, I don't think so. I mean, I feel like I stay on topic just fine. When I'm talking to my staff or anyone else, I know what I want to say and I just say it. I haven't noticed any issues with getting sidetracked or anything like that. Like I said, my own thinking and talking seem pretty normal to me.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever have thoughts that don't make sense to you or seem disconnected from each other?\n\nPatient: No, not really. My thoughts feel connected. I don't have moments where my own ideas don't make sense to me. The things I think about are normal... it's just the other things, like the voices, that don't make sense. But my actual thoughts are fine.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed doing anything else out of character or unusual besides rearranging things and repeating phrases in meetings and events?\n\nPatient: Well, besides what we just talked about... sometimes I'll just suddenly stand up in the middle of a meeting. Or I'll hold my hands up in the air, kind of like I'm presenting something to everyone. It's strange because, afterward, I know it made absolutely no sense for the room or what was going on. But in the moment, I just do it.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever have trouble understanding what's being said to you because your mind is somewhere else?\n\nPatient: No, I don't think so. When people are talking to me, I hear what they're saying. I might be distracted by... well, the other things going on, but I still understand the words they're saying to me. My mind doesn't really drift off like that.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever have thoughts that feel like they're not connected to each other in a logical way?\n\nPatient: No, I don't think so. I mean, you kind of just asked me that, right? My thoughts feel fine. They connect to each other the way they always have. It's just... the voices and the things I do that are strange, not the thoughts themselves.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever have difficulty following your own train of thought?\n\nPatient: No, I don't have trouble with that. That's not something that happens to me.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever have trouble knowing what you want to say before you start talking?\n\nPatient: No, not really. When people are talking to me, I hear what they're saying. I might be distracted by... well, the other things going on, but I still understand the words they're saying to me. My mind doesn't really drift off like that.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever have thoughts that feel like they're not connected to each other in a logical way?\n\nPatient: No, I don't think so. I mean, you kind of just asked me that, right? My thoughts feel fine. They connect to each other the way they always have. It's just... the voices and the things I do that are strange, not the thoughts themselves.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever have difficulty following your own train of thought?\n\nPatient: No, I don't have trouble with that. If I start thinking about a problem at work or... whatever it is, I can follow it through to the end just fine. My train of thought is clear to me. Like I said, it's really just the other things happening around me that are the issue, not my actual thinking.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever have thoughts that feel like they're being taken out of your mind or broadcasted to others?\n\nPatient: No, I've never experienced anything like that. My thoughts are my own... they stay in my head. I don't feel like anyone is taking them or broadcasting them for other people to hear. That's not something that happens to me.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever have trouble knowing what you want to say before you start talking?\n\nPatient: No, not really. I mean, sometimes I might pause for a second to find the right word, like anyone else, but I don't struggle to know what I want to say. I generally know exactly what I need to communicate before I open my mouth. It's not a problem I have.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever have thoughts that feel like they're not your own?\n\nPatient: No, I don't have that either. Like I was saying before, my thoughts are entirely my own. I've never felt like... like someone else was putting them there, or that they belonged to another person. Everything in my head is just my own thinking.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you've been showing less emotion than usual?\n\nPatient: Yes, I suppose I have. I used to be the person driving every idea, you know, calling people right after openings and being really involved. But now... I can just sit at my desk for hours staring at the agenda. I barely speak to my husband or my staff anymore. I just feel almost nothing about the work I used to really love. I don't really react to things the way I used to.\n\nDoctor: Is this feeling of showing less emotion than usual something that happens all the time now?\n\nPatient: Yeah, pretty much. I mean, I haven't really tracked it to see if it's every single minute, but... it feels constant. From the dialogue so far and your current candidates, ask the one follow-up question that best separates them.\n\n[Current Turn]\n19\n\n[Current Candidates]\nF22, F23, F25.1\n\n[Dialogue History]\nDoctor: Hello, welcome to the clinic. What brings you in to see me today?\n\nPatient: I came in because... well, I keep hearing people talking about me. It's usually board members or my staff. I hear them through the conference room walls, or even from my phone when it's completely turned off. They're always saying things like I'm ruining the company. It sounds so incredibly clear that I'll actually answer them out loud... and then I realize no one else in the room heard a thing.\n\nDoctor: Do you believe that the voices you're hearing are real?\n\nPatient: Yes. I mean, like I just mentioned, they sound completely real to me. When I hear the board members or my staff through the conference room walls, or coming from my phone even when it's turned off, there's no difference between that and a normal conversation. \n\nI hear them saying I'm ruining the company, and the voices are so clear that I actually answer them back. It's only after I speak out loud and look around to realize no one else in the room heard a thing that I get confused. I mean... I haven't noticed anything like that. They just say those things about how I'm ruining the company, but I don't feel like they're planning a conspiracy or anything dangerous. It's just... the talking.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever find it hard to express your thoughts or have trouble organizing your thoughts when talking to others?\n\nPatient: No, I haven't noticed anything like that. I mean... I talk to people all day for my job, directing my staff and running meetings. I don't have any trouble putting my sentences together or explaining what I want to say. My own thoughts are perfectly clear to me... it's really just hearing those voices that's the issue.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any changes in your behavior or the way you act that seem unusual or out of character for you?\n\nPatient: Yes, I actually have. It's... well, it happens mostly in meetings, or sometimes at our gallery events. I'll just suddenly stand up. And I'll start rearranging things on the table—like putting pens or people's name cards into these specific patterns. \n\nOr, um, someone will say something, and I'll just repeat the exact phrase they just said out loud. Sometimes I even hold my hands up in the air, like I'm presenting something to the room, but I'm not holding anything. In the moment I just do it, but afterward... I mean, I know it makes absolutely no sense for the situation. It's definitely very out of character for me.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever feel like your thoughts are racing or jumping from one idea to another in a way that's hard to control?\n\nPatient: No, not really. I mean, my thoughts don't feel like they're racing or jumping around out of control. Like I said before, my own thoughts feel pretty clear to me. It's just the voices that get in the way, but my actual thinking process... it feels normal. I don't feel like I'm losing control of my own ideas.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever find it hard to stay on topic when you're talking to someone?\n\nPatient: No, I don't think so. I mean, I feel like I stay on topic just fine. When I'm talking to my staff or anyone else, I know what I want to say and I just say it. I haven't noticed any issues with getting sidetracked or anything like that. Like I said, my own thinking and talking seem pretty normal to me.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever have thoughts that don't make sense to you or seem disconnected from each other?\n\nPatient: No, not really. My thoughts feel connected. I don't have moments where my own ideas don't make sense to me. The things I think about are normal... it's just the other things, like the voices, that don't make sense. But my actual thoughts are fine.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed doing anything else out of character or unusual besides rearranging things and repeating phrases in meetings and events?\n\nPatient: Well, besides what we just talked about... sometimes I'll just suddenly stand up in the middle of a meeting. Or I'll hold my hands up in the air, kind of like I'm presenting something to everyone. It's strange because, afterward, I know it made absolutely no sense for the room or what was going on. But in the moment, I just do it.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever have trouble understanding what's being said to you because your mind is somewhere else?\n\nPatient: No, I don't think so. When people are talking to me, I hear what they're saying. I might be distracted by... well, the other things going on, but I still understand the words they're saying to me. My mind doesn't really drift off like that.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever have thoughts that feel like they're not connected to each other in a logical way?\n\nPatient: No, I don't think so. I mean, you kind of just asked me that, right? My thoughts feel fine. They connect to each other the way they always have. It's just... the voices and the things I do that are strange, not the thoughts themselves.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever have difficulty following your own train of thought?\n\nPatient: No, I don't have trouble with that. If I start thinking about a problem at work or... whatever it is, I can follow it through to the end just fine. My train of thought is clear to me. Like I said, it's really just the other things happening around me that are the issue, not my actual thinking.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever have thoughts that feel like they're being taken out of your mind or broadcasted to others?\n\nPatient: No, I've never experienced anything like that. My thoughts are my own... they stay in my head. I don't feel like anyone is taking them or broadcasting them for other people to hear. That's not something that happens to me.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever have trouble knowing what you want to say before you start talking?\n\nPatient: No, not really. I mean, sometimes I might pause for a second to find the right word, like anyone else, but I don't struggle to know what I want to say. I generally know exactly what I need to communicate before I open my mouth. It's not a problem I have.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever have thoughts that feel like they're not your own?\n\nPatient: No, I don't have that either. Like I was saying before, my thoughts are entirely my own. I've never felt like... like someone else was putting them there, or that they belonged to another person. Everything in my head is just my own thinking.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you've been showing less emotion than usual?\n\nPatient: Yes, I suppose I have. I used to be the person driving every idea, you know, calling people right after openings and being really involved. But now... I can just sit at my desk for hours staring at the agenda. I barely speak to my husband or my staff anymore. I just feel almost nothing about the work I used to really love. I don't really react to things the way I used to.\n\nDoctor: Is this feeling of showing less emotion than usual something that happens all the time now?\n\nPatient: Yeah, pretty much. I mean, I haven't really tracked it to see if it's every single minute, but... it feels constant. I don't really have moments where I suddenly feel like my old self or get excited about things anymore. It's just kind of how I am every day now.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel sad or empty most of the time?\n\nPatient: No, not really sad. Empty... I guess, maybe? But it's not like a depressed kind of empty. It's just... I just don't feel much of anything. I wouldn't call it sadness, though.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Ask about exactly ONE thing. Like I said, my own thinking and talking seem pretty normal to me.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever have thoughts that don't make sense to you or seem disconnected from each other?\n\nPatient: No, not really. My thoughts feel connected. I don't have moments where my own ideas don't make sense to me. The things I think about are normal... it's just the other things, like the voices, that don't make sense. But my actual thoughts are fine.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed doing anything else out of character or unusual besides rearranging things and repeating phrases in meetings and events?\n\nPatient: Well, besides what we just talked about... sometimes I'll just suddenly stand up in the middle of a meeting. Or I'll hold my hands up in the air, kind of like I'm presenting something to everyone. It's strange because, afterward, I know it made absolutely no sense for the room or what was going on. But in the moment, I just do it.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever have trouble understanding what's being said to you because your mind is somewhere else?\n\nPatient: No, I don't think so. When people are talking to me, I hear what they're saying. I might be distracted by... well, the other things going on, but I still understand the words they're saying to me. My mind doesn't really drift off like that.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever have thoughts that feel like they're not connected to each other in a logical way?\n\nPatient: No, I don't think so. I mean, you kind of just asked me that, right? My thoughts feel fine. They connect to each other the way they always have. It's just... the voices and the things I do that are strange, not the thoughts themselves.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever have difficulty following your own train of thought?\n\nPatient: No, I don't have trouble with that. If I start thinking about a problem at work or... whatever it is, I can follow it through to the end just fine. My train of thought is clear to me. Like I said, it's really just the other things happening around me that are the issue, not my actual thinking.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever have thoughts that feel like they're being taken out of your mind or broadcasted to others?\n\nPatient: No, I've never experienced anything like that. My thoughts are my own... they stay in my head. I don't feel like anyone is taking them or broadcasting them for other people to hear. That's not something that happens to me.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever have trouble knowing what you want to say before you start talking?\n\nPatient: No, not really. I mean, sometimes I might pause for a second to find the right word, like anyone else, but I don't struggle to know what I want to say. I generally know exactly what I need to communicate before I open my mouth. It's not a problem I have.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever have thoughts that feel like they're not your own?\n\nPatient: No, I don't have that either. Like I was saying before, my thoughts are entirely my own. I've never felt like... like someone else was putting them there, or that they belonged to another person. Everything in my head is just my own thinking.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you've been showing less emotion than usual?\n\nPatient: Yes, I suppose I have. I used to be the person driving every idea, you know, calling people right after openings and being really involved. But now... I can just sit at my desk for hours staring at the agenda. I barely speak to my husband or my staff anymore. I just feel almost nothing about the work I used to really love. I don't really react to things the way I used to.\n\nDoctor: Is this feeling of showing less emotion than usual something that happens all the time now?\n\nPatient: Yeah, pretty much. I mean, I haven't really tracked it to see if it's every single minute, but... it feels constant. I don't really have moments where I suddenly feel like my old self or get excited about things anymore. It's just kind of how I am every day now.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel sad or empty most of the time?\n\nPatient: No, not really sad. Empty... I guess, maybe? But it's not like a depressed kind of empty. It's just... I just don't feel much of anything. I wouldn't call it sadness, though.\n\nDoctor: Is this feeling of not really reacting to things or feeling much of anything something that you experience every single day?\n\nPatient: Yes, it is every day. It's just... the voices and the things I do that are strange, not the thoughts themselves.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever have difficulty following your own train of thought?\n\nPatient: No, I don't have trouble with that. If I start thinking about a problem at work or... whatever it is, I can follow it through to the end just fine. My train of thought is clear to me. Like I said, it's really just the other things happening around me that are the issue, not my actual thinking.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever have thoughts that feel like they're being taken out of your mind or broadcasted to others?\n\nPatient: No, I've never experienced anything like that. My thoughts are my own... they stay in my head. I don't feel like anyone is taking them or broadcasting them for other people to hear. That's not something that happens to me.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever have trouble knowing what you want to say before you start talking?\n\nPatient: No, not really. I mean, sometimes I might pause for a second to find the right word, like anyone else, but I don't struggle to know what I want to say. I generally know exactly what I need to communicate before I open my mouth. It's not a problem I have.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever have thoughts that feel like they're not your own?\n\nPatient: No, I don't have that either. Like I was saying before, my thoughts are entirely my own. I've never felt like... like someone else was putting them there, or that they belonged to another person. Everything in my head is just my own thinking.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you've been showing less emotion than usual?\n\nPatient: Yes, I suppose I have. I used to be the person driving every idea, you know, calling people right after openings and being really involved. But now... I can just sit at my desk for hours staring at the agenda. I barely speak to my husband or my staff anymore. I just feel almost nothing about the work I used to really love. I don't really react to things the way I used to.\n\nDoctor: Is this feeling of showing less emotion than usual something that happens all the time now?\n\nPatient: Yeah, pretty much. I mean, I haven't really tracked it to see if it's every single minute, but... it feels constant. I don't really have moments where I suddenly feel like my old self or get excited about things anymore. It's just kind of how I am every day now.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel sad or empty most of the time?\n\nPatient: No, not really sad. Empty... I guess, maybe? But it's not like a depressed kind of empty. It's just... I just don't feel much of anything. I wouldn't call it sadness, though.\n\nDoctor: Is this feeling of not really reacting to things or feeling much of anything something that you experience every single day?\n\nPatient: Yes, it is every day. I don't... I don't think there's been a single day recently where I felt any different. It's just always exactly like this.\n\nDoctor: Do you think your strange behaviors, like rearranging things or repeating phrases, are connected to the voices you hear?\n\nPatient: I don't... I'm not really sure what you mean by that. I don't rearrange things or repeat phrases. I mean, I hear the voices, yes, but I don't do those other things you're talking about. So... no, there isn't any connection like that."
